[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/ALSEP_AS17-134-20500.jpg/500px-ALSEP_AS17-134-20500.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ALSEP_AS17-134-20500.jpg) Artifacts of human activity, [Apollo 17's Lunar Surface Experiments Package](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Lunar_Surface_Experiments_Package "Apollo Lunar Surface Experiments Package")[&#8202;](#ref-back-Garber_2012-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-Garber_2012)

While the Moon has the lowest [planetary protection target-categorization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planetary_protection#Target_categories "Planetary protection"), its degradation as a pristine body and scientific place has been discussed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Vidaurri_2019-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-Vidaurri_2019) If there is [astronomy performed from the Moon](#Astronomy_from_the_Moon), it will need to be free from any physical and [radio pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_pollution "Radio pollution"). While the Moon has no significant atmosphere, traffic and impacts on the Moon causes clouds of dust that can spread far and possibly contaminate the original state of the Moon and its special scientific content.[&#8202;](#ref-back-David_2020-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-David_2020) Scholar [Alice Gorman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_Gorman "Alice Gorman") asserts that, although the Moon is inhospitable, describing the Moon as dead negates its dynamics, which requires sustainable human activity to treat the Moon's ecology as a co-participant.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Gorman_2022-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-Gorman_2022)

The so-called " [Tardigrade affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tardigrades_on_the_Moon "Tardigrades on the Moon")" of the 2019 crashed _[Beresheet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beresheet "Beresheet")_ lander and its carrying of [tardigrades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tardigrade "Tardigrade") has been discussed as an example for lacking measures and lacking international regulation for [planetary protection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planetary_protection "Planetary protection").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Alvarez_2020_p.-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-Alvarez_2020_p.)

[Space debris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_debris "Space debris") beyond Earth around the Moon has been considered as a future challenge with increasing numbers of missions to the Moon, particularly as a danger for such missions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Carter_2022-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-Carter_2022)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Nast_2013-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-Nast_2013) As such lunar waste management has been raised as an issue which future lunar missions, particularly on the surface, need to tackle.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Pino_Salmeri_Hugo_Hume_p.-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-Pino_Salmeri_Hugo_Hume_p.)[&#8202;](#ref-back-NASA/ADS-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-NASA/ADS)

Human remains have been transported to the Moon, including by private companies such as [Celestis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celestis "Celestis") and [Elysium Space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elysium_Space "Elysium Space"). Because the Moon has been sacred or significant to many cultures, the practice of [space burials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_burial "Space burial") have attracted criticism from [indigenous peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples "Indigenous peoples") leaders. For example, then– [Navajo Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo_Nation "Navajo Nation") president [Albert Hale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Hale "Albert Hale") criticized NASA for sending the [cremated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cremated "Cremated") ashes of scientist [Eugene Shoemaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_Shoemaker "Eugene Shoemaker") to the Moon in 1998.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Magazine_Sullivan_2024_a329-0)[\[348\]](#refnote-Magazine_Sullivan_2024_a329)[&#8202;](#ref-back-web.archive.org_2011_c901-0)[\[349\]](#refnote-web.archive.org_2011_c901)

Beside the remains of human activity on the Moon, there have been some intended permanent installations like the _[Moon Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Museum "Moon Museum")_ art piece, [Apollo 11 goodwill messages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11_goodwill_messages "Apollo 11 goodwill messages"), six [lunar plaques](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_plaque "Lunar plaque"), the _[Fallen Astronaut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fallen_Astronaut "Fallen Astronaut")_ memorial, and other artifacts.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Garber_2012-1)[\[339\]](#refnote-Garber_2012)

### Astronomy from the Moon

Further information: [Extraterrestrial sky §The Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraterrestrial_sky#The_Moon "Extraterrestrial sky")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Earth%E2%80%99s_geocorona_from_the_Moon.jpg/500px-Earth%E2%80%99s_geocorona_from_the_Moon.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Earth%E2%80%99s_geocorona_from_the_Moon.jpg) Earth's [exosphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exosphere "Exosphere") illuminated creating its [geocorona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geocorona "Geocorona"), visible in ultraviolet and viewed by the [Far Ultraviolet Camera/Spectrograph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_Ultraviolet_Camera/Spectrograph "Far Ultraviolet Camera/Spectrograph") of [Apollo 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_16 "Apollo 16") in 1972 from the Moon's surface

The Moon has been used as a site for astronomical and [Earth observations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earth_observation "Earth observation"). The Earth appears in the [Moon's sky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraterrestrial_sky#The_Moon "Extraterrestrial sky") with an [apparent size](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apparent_size "Apparent size") of 1°48′ to 2°,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Gorkavyi_Krotkov_Marshak_2023_pp._1527–1537-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-Gorkavyi_Krotkov_Marshak_2023_pp._1527–1537) three to four times the size of the Moon or Sun in Earth's sky, or about the apparent width of two little fingers at an arm's length away. Observations from the Moon started as early as 1966 with the [first images of Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_first_images_of_Earth_from_space "Timeline of first images of Earth from space") from the Moon, taken by [Lunar Orbiter 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_Orbiter_1 "Lunar Orbiter 1"). Of particular cultural significance is the 1968 photograph called _[Earthrise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthrise "Earthrise")_, taken by [Bill Anders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Anders "Bill Anders") of [Apollo 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_8 "Apollo 8") in 1968. In April 1972 the [Apollo 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_16 "Apollo 16") mission set up the first dedicated telescope,[&#8202;](#ref-back-w422-0)[\[351\]](#refnote-w422)[&#8202;](#ref-back-x909-0)[\[352\]](#refnote-x909) the [Far Ultraviolet Camera/Spectrograph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_Ultraviolet_Camera/Spectrograph "Far Ultraviolet Camera/Spectrograph"), recording various astronomical photos and spectra.[&#8202;](#ref-back-365-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-365)

The Moon is recognized as an excellent site for telescopes.[&#8202;](#ref-back-366-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-366) It is relatively nearby; certain craters near the poles are permanently dark and cold and especially useful for [infrared telescopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrared_telescope "Infrared telescope"); and [radio telescopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_telescope "Radio telescope") on the far side would be shielded from the radio chatter of Earth.[&#8202;](#ref-back-367-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-367) The [lunar soil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_soil "Lunar soil"), although it poses a problem for any moving parts of [telescopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telescope "Telescope"), can be mixed with [carbon nanotubes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_nanotube "Carbon nanotube") and [epoxies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epoxy "Epoxy") and employed in the construction of mirrors up to 50 meters in diameter.[&#8202;](#ref-back-368-0)[\[356\]](#refnote-368) A lunar [zenith telescope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zenith_telescope "Zenith telescope") can be made cheaply with an [ionic liquid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_liquid "Ionic liquid").[&#8202;](#ref-back-369-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-369)

With increased human activity on the surface of the Moon the atmospheric circulation of lunar dust will increase, reducing the favorable conditions for astronomy from the surface depending on measures to mitigate the spread of lunar dust.[&#8202;](#ref-back-y615-0)[\[358\]](#refnote-y615)

### Proposed missions

See also: [List of proposed missions to the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_proposed_missions_to_the_Moon "List of proposed missions to the Moon")

Both the U.S. and Chinese lunar programs have the goal to establish a [lunar base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_base "Lunar base") in the 2030s with their international partners, being the first crewed lunar landings since [Apollo 17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_17 "Apollo 17") in 1972.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Since_Apollo_17-0)[\[359\]](#refnote-Since_Apollo_17)

Following the [Artemis III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis_III "Artemis III") test flight in [low Earth orbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low_Earth_orbit "Low Earth orbit") scheduled for 2027, NASA plans to return humans to the lunar surface in 2028, with two [Artemis IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis_IV "Artemis IV") astronauts landing on the lunar south pole.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Since_Apollo_17-1)[\[359\]](#refnote-Since_Apollo_17) While the Apollo missions were explorational in nature, the Artemis program plans to establish a more permanent presence. To this end, the U.S. is partnering with industry for lunar communications, payload delivery, and [in situ resource utilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_situ_resource_utilization "In situ resource utilization") through programs such as NASA's [Commercial Lunar Payload Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_Lunar_Payload_Services "Commercial Lunar Payload Services"), and [DARPA lunar programs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DARPA_lunar_programs "DARPA lunar programs").[&#8202;](#ref-back-372-0)[\[360\]](#refnote-372)[&#8202;](#ref-back-373-0)[\[361\]](#refnote-373)[&#8202;](#ref-back-374-0)[\[362\]](#refnote-374)

China's [Chang'e program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang'e_program "Chang'e program") announced joint missions with Russia's [Luna-Glob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna-Glob "Luna-Glob") program.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TASS_2019_x202-0)[\[363\]](#refnote-TASS_2019_x202)[&#8202;](#ref-back-376-0)[\[364\]](#refnote-376) Two lunar survey missions are planned, [Chang'e 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang'e_7 "Chang'e 7") in 2026 and [Chang'e 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang'e_8 "Chang'e 8") in 2028, before a crewed landing in 2029 or 2030.[&#8202;](#ref-back-377-0)[\[365\]](#refnote-377)[&#8202;](#ref-back-378-0)[\[366\]](#refnote-378)

### Living on the Moon

Main article: [Lunar habitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_habitation "Lunar habitation")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Aldrin_Looks_Back_at_Tranquility_Base_-_GPN-2000-001102.jpg/500px-Aldrin_Looks_Back_at_Tranquility_Base_-_GPN-2000-001102.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Aldrin_Looks_Back_at_Tranquility_Base_-_GPN-2000-001102.jpg) Astronaut [Buzz Aldrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buzz_Aldrin "Buzz Aldrin") in [life-supporting suit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo/Skylab_spacesuit "Apollo/Skylab spacesuit") looking back at the first [lunar habitat and base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_base "Lunar base"), the [Lunar Module _Eagle_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_Module_Eagle "Lunar Module Eagle") of [Tranquility Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tranquility_Base "Tranquility Base"), during [Apollo 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11 "Apollo 11") (1969), the first crewed [Moon landing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_landing "Moon landing")

Humans have lived on the Moon in groups of two and for up to three days.[&#8202;](#ref-back-i632-0)[\[367\]](#refnote-i632) Altogether twelve people have stayed on the Moon over the course of six visits.[&#8202;](#ref-back-n138-0)[\[368\]](#refnote-n138) They all lived in one type of [surface habitat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_habitat_(facility)#Extraterrestrial_surface_habitat "Space habitat (facility)"), the [Apollo Lunar Module](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Lunar_Module "Apollo Lunar Module").[&#8202;](#ref-back-381-0)[\[369\]](#refnote-381) In the course of these stays some people have spent in total up to one day roaming the surface.[&#8202;](#ref-back-i632-1)[\[367\]](#refnote-i632)

Challenges when roaming the surface arise from [lunar dust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_dust "Lunar dust") sticking to suits and tools, and has been carried inside the habitats. Astronauts could taste and smell the dust, which smells like gunpowder and was called the "Apollo aroma".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Aroma-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-Aroma) This fine lunar dust can [cause health issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_dust#Harmful_effects_of_lunar_dust "Lunar dust").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Aroma-1)[\[370\]](#refnote-Aroma)

In 2019, at least one plant seed sprouted in an experiment on the [Chang'e 4 lander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_4#Lunar_lander "Change 4"). It was carried from Earth along with other small life in its _Lunar Micro Ecosystem_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Seeds-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-Seeds)

Because the Moon is tidally locked, and only shows one face to Earth, a permanent lunar presence will require a constellation of satellites to relay signals from the Moon's far side, as well as over its topographically inhibitive south pole. The Chinese [Queqiao-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queqiao-1 "Queqiao-1") [and -2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queqiao-2 "Queqiao-2") satellites are already in place, and NASA plans the [Lunar Communications Relay and Navigation Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LunaNet "LunaNet") (LCRNS) for November 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-384-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-384)[&#8202;](#ref-back-385-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-385)

### Legal status

See also: [Space law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_law "Space law"), [Politics of outer space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_outer_space "Politics of outer space"), [Space advocacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_advocacy "Space advocacy"), [Colonization of the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonization_of_the_Moon "Colonization of the Moon"), [Outer Space Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outer_Space_Treaty "Outer Space Treaty"), and [Moon Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Treaty "Moon Treaty")

Although _[Luna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_program "Luna program")_ landers scattered pennants of the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union "Soviet Union") on the Moon, and [U.S. flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_Flag_Assembly "Lunar Flag Assembly") were symbolically planted at their landing sites by the [Apollo astronauts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_astronauts "Apollo astronauts"), no nation claims ownership of any part of the Moon's surface.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unoosa_q6-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-unoosa_q6) Likewise no [private ownership of parts of the Moon, or as a whole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraterrestrial_real_estate "Extraterrestrial real estate"), is considered credible.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unoosa_q7-0)[\[375\]](#refnote-unoosa_q7)[&#8202;](#ref-back-iisl_2004-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-iisl_2004)[&#8202;](#ref-back-iisl_2009-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-iisl_2009)

The 1967 [Outer Space Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outer_Space_Treaty "Outer Space Treaty") defines the Moon and all outer space as the " [province of all mankind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_heritage_of_mankind "Common heritage of mankind")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-unoosa_q6-1)[\[374\]](#refnote-unoosa_q6) It restricts the use of the Moon to peaceful purposes, explicitly banning military installations and [weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapons_of_mass_destruction "Weapons of mass destruction").[&#8202;](#ref-back-unoosa_q5-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-unoosa_q5) A majority of countries are parties of this treaty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unoosa_q4-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-unoosa_q4)
The 1979 [Moon Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Agreement "Moon Agreement") was created to elaborate, and restrict the exploitation of the [Moon's resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_resources "Lunar resources") by any single nation, leaving it to a yet unspecified international regulatory regime.[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_Space_Review_2021-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-The_Space_Review_2021) As of January 2020, it has been signed and ratified by 18 nations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unoosa_moon-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-unoosa_moon)

Since 2020, countries have joined the U.S. in their [Artemis Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis_Accords "Artemis Accords"), which are challenging the treaty. The U.S. has furthermore emphasized in a presidential [executive order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_order "Executive order") ("Encouraging International Support for the Recovery and Use of Space Resources.") that "the United States does not view outer space as a 'global commons'" and calls the Moon Agreement "a failed attempt at constraining free enterprise".[&#8202;](#ref-back-394-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-394)[&#8202;](#ref-back-395-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-395)

With Australia signing and ratifying both the Moon Treaty in 1986 as well as the Artemis Accords in 2020, there has been a discussion if they can be harmonized.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Australian_Institute_of_International_Affairs_2021-1)[\[325\]](#refnote-Australian_Institute_of_International_Affairs_2021) In this light an _Implementation Agreement_ for the Moon Treaty has been advocated for, as a way to compensate for the shortcomings of the Moon Treaty and to harmonize it with other laws and agreements such as the Artemis Accords, allowing it to be more widely accepted.[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_Space_Review_2020-1)[\[324\]](#refnote-The_Space_Review_2020)[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_Space_Treaty_Institute_–_Dedicated_to_Peace_and_Sustainability_in_Outer_Space._Our_Mission-1)[\[326\]](#refnote-The_Space_Treaty_Institute_–_Dedicated_to_Peace_and_Sustainability_in_Outer_Space._Our_Mission)

In the face of such increasing commercial and national interest, particularly prospecting territories, U.S. lawmakers have introduced in late 2020 specific regulation for the conservation of historic landing sites[&#8202;](#ref-back-HowStuffWorks_2021-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-HowStuffWorks_2021) and interest groups have argued for making such sites [World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site "World Heritage Site")[&#8202;](#ref-back-For_All_Moonkind-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-For_All_Moonkind) and zones of scientific value protected zones, all of which add to the legal availability and territorialization of the Moon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Alvarez_2020_p.-1)[\[343\]](#refnote-Alvarez_2020_p.)

In 2021, the _Declaration of the Rights of the Moon_[&#8202;](#ref-back-Declaration-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-Declaration) was created by a group of "lawyers, space archaeologists and concerned citizens", drawing on precedents in the [Rights of Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rights_of_Nature "Rights of Nature") movement and the concept of legal personality for non-human entities in space.[&#8202;](#ref-back-399-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-399)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Evans_2021-0)[\[388\]](#refnote-Evans_2021)

#### Coordination and regulation

Increasing human activity at the Moon has raised the need for coordination to safeguard international and commercial lunar activity. Issues from cooperation to mere coordination, through for example the development of a shared [Lunar time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_time "Lunar time"), have been raised.>[&#8202;](#ref-back-401-0)[\[389\]](#refnote-401)

In particular the establishment of an [international](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internationalism_(politics) "Internationalism (politics)") or [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations "United Nations") regulatory regime for lunar human activity has been called for by the [Moon Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Treaty "Moon Treaty") and suggested through an _Implementation Agreement_,[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_Space_Review_2020-2)[\[324\]](#refnote-The_Space_Review_2020)[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_Space_Treaty_Institute_–_Dedicated_to_Peace_and_Sustainability_in_Outer_Space._Our_Mission-2)[\[326\]](#refnote-The_Space_Treaty_Institute_–_Dedicated_to_Peace_and_Sustainability_in_Outer_Space._Our_Mission) but remains contentious. Current lunar programs are [multilateral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilateralism "Multilateralism"), with the US-led [Artemis program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis_program "Artemis program") and the China-led [International Lunar Research Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Lunar_Research_Station "International Lunar Research Station"). For broader international cooperation and coordination, the [International Lunar Exploration Working Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Lunar_Exploration_Working_Group "International Lunar Exploration Working Group") (ILEWG), the [Moon Village Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Village "Moon Village") (MVA) and more generally the International Space Exploration Coordination Group (ISECG) has been established.[&#8202;](#ref-back-402-0)[\[390\]](#refnote-402)

## In culture and life

### Mythology

[Lunar deities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_deity "Lunar deity") are common across all human cultures. Many cultures have implicitly linked the 29.5-day [lunar cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_cycle "Lunar cycle") to women's [menstrual cycles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menstrual_cycle "Menstrual cycle"), as evident in the shared linguistic roots of "menstruation" and "moon" words in multiple [language families](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_family "Language family").[&#8202;](#ref-back-403-0)[\[391\]](#refnote-403) This identification was not universal, as demonstrated by the fact that not all moon deities are female. Still, many well-known mythologies feature moon goddesses, including the Greek goddess [Selene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selene "Selene"), the Roman goddess [Luna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_(mythology) "Luna (mythology)"), the Chinese goddess [Chang'e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang'e "Chang'e"), and the Aztec goddess [Coyolxāuhqui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coyolx%C4%81uhqui "Coyolxāuhqui"), whose decapitation may represent a lunar eclipse.[&#8202;](#ref-back-404-0)[\[392\]](#refnote-404) Some male gods like Nanna-Sin where linked to childbirth and pregnancy as well.[&#8202;](#ref-back-405-0)[\[393\]](#refnote-405) Several goddesses including [Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis"), [Hecate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hecate "Hecate"), [Melinoë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melino%C3%AB "Melinoë"), [Phoebe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoebe_(Titaness) "Phoebe (Titaness)"), [Theia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theia "Theia") and [Isis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isis "Isis") did not originally have lunar aspects, and only acquired them late in antiquity due to [syncretism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syncretism "Syncretism") with the de facto Greco-Roman lunar deity Selene/ [Luna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_(goddess) "Luna (goddess)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-406-0)[\[394\]](#refnote-406)[&#8202;](#ref-back-407-0)[\[395\]](#refnote-407)

The [splitting of the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Splitting_of_the_Moon "Splitting of the Moon") into two halves in the sky is a miracle attributed to the prophet [Mohammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad "Mohammad").[&#8202;](#ref-back-408-0)[\[396\]](#refnote-408)

### Timekeeping

Further information: [Lunar calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_calendar "Lunar calendar"), [Lunisolar calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunisolar_calendar "Lunisolar calendar"), and [Metonic cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metonic_cycle "Metonic cycle")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Venus-de-Laussel-detail-corne.jpg/500px-Venus-de-Laussel-detail-corne.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Venus-de-Laussel-detail-corne.jpg) The [Venus of Laussel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venus_of_Laussel "Venus of Laussel") (c. 25,000 [BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Before_present "Before present")) holding a crescent shaped horn. The 13 notches on the horn may symbolize the average number of days from menstruation to an [ovulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovulation "Ovulation"), or the approximate number of full [menstrual cycles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menstrual_cycle "Menstrual cycle") and [lunar cycles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_cycle "Lunar cycle") per year (although these two phenomena are unrelated).[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0b-0)[\[397\]](#refnote-:0b)[&#8202;](#ref-back-e093-1)[\[255\]](#refnote-e093)

Since prehistoric times people have taken note of [the Moon's phases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_phases "Lunar phases") and its [waxing and waning cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_cycle "Lunar cycle") and used it to keep record of time.
The words for the month in a range of different languages carry this relation between the period of the month and the Moon etymologically.
This lunar timekeeping gave rise to the historically dominant, but varied, [lunisolar calendars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunisolar_calendar "Lunisolar calendar"). The 7th-century [Islamic calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_calendar "Islamic calendar") is an example of a purely [lunar calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_calendar "Lunar calendar"), where months are traditionally determined by the visual sighting of the hilal, or earliest crescent moon, over the horizon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-410-0)[\[398\]](#refnote-410)

Of particular significance has been the occasion of [full moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Full_moon "Full moon"), highlighted and celebrated in a range of calendars and cultures, an example being the Buddhist [Vesak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vesak "Vesak"). The full moon around the [southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_equinox "March equinox") or [northern autumnal equinox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_equinox "September equinox") is often called the [harvest moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvest_moon "Harvest moon") and is celebrated with festivities such as the [Harvest Moon Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvest_Moon_Festival "Harvest Moon Festival") of the [Chinese lunar calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_lunar_calendar "Chinese lunar calendar"), its second most important celebration after [the Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_New_Year "Chinese New Year") lunisolar [Lunar New Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_New_Year "Lunar New Year").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Confucius_Institute_for_Scotland_2022-0)[\[399\]](#refnote-Confucius_Institute_for_Scotland_2022)

### Cultural representation

Further information: [Cultural astronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_astronomy "Cultural astronomy"), [Archaeoastronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeoastronomy "Archaeoastronomy"), [Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent "Crescent"), and [Man in the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_in_the_Moon "Man in the Moon")

See also: [Nocturne (painting)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nocturne_(painting) "Nocturne (painting)") and [Moon magic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_magic "Moon magic")

Recurring lunar [aspects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspect_(religion) "Aspect (religion)") of [lunar deities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_deities "Lunar deities")

[![Sumerian cylinder seal and impression, dated c. 2100 BC, of Ḫašḫamer, ensi (governor) of Iškun-Sin c. 2100 BC. The seated figure is probably king Ur-Nammu, bestowing the governorship on Ḫašḫamer, who is led before him by Lamma (protective goddess).](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Sumerian_Cylinder_Seal_of_King_Ur-Nammu.jpg/500px-Sumerian_Cylinder_Seal_of_King_Ur-Nammu.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sumerian_Cylinder_Seal_of_King_Ur-Nammu.jpg)

The [crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent "Crescent") of [Nanna/Sîn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sin_(mythology) "Sin (mythology)"), c.2100 BC

[![Luna on the Parabiago plate (2nd–5th century), featuring the crescent crown, chariot and velificatio as lunar aspect found in different cultures.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Patera_di_Parabiago_-_MI_-_Museo_archeologico_-_Diana_-_Luna_-_25-7-2003_-_Foto_Giovanni_Dall%27Orto_-_25-7-2003.jpg/500px-Patera_di_Parabiago_-_MI_-_Museo_archeologico_-_Diana_-_Luna_-_25-7-2003_-_Foto_Giovanni_Dall%27Orto_-_25-7-2003.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Patera_di_Parabiago_-_MI_-_Museo_archeologico_-_Diana_-_Luna_-_25-7-2003_-_Foto_Giovanni_Dall'Orto_-_25-7-2003.jpg)

Crescent headgear, [chariot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_(goddess)#Chariot_of_the_Moon "Luna (goddess)") and [velificatio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velificatio "Velificatio") of [Luna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_(goddess) "Luna (goddess)"), 2nd–5th century

[![Rabbits are in a range of cultures identified with the Moon, from China to the Indigenous peoples of the Americas, as with the rabbit (on the left) of the Maya moon goddess (6th–9th century).](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/ad/Goddess_O_Ixchel.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Goddess_O_Ixchel.jpg)

A [Moon rabbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_rabbit "Moon rabbit") of the [Maya moon goddess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maya_moon_goddess "Maya moon goddess"), 6th–9th century

Humans have depicted the Moon since [prehistoric times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistoric_times "Prehistoric times"), as evidenced by visual depictions from 40,000 [BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Before_present "Before present") and written depictions from the [4th millennium BCE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_millennium_BCE "4th millennium BCE"). The oldest named astronomer and poet [Enheduanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enheduanna "Enheduanna"), [Akkadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akkadian_Empire "Akkadian Empire") high priestess to the lunar deity [Nanna/Sin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sin_(mythology) "Sin (mythology)") and daughter of [Sargon the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sargon_the_Great "Sargon the Great") (c.2334 – c.2279 BCE), tracked the Moon and wrote poems about her.[&#8202;](#ref-back-c099-1)[\[260\]](#refnote-c099)

#### Crescent

For the representation of the Moon, especially its [lunar phases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_phase "Lunar phase"), the [crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent "Crescent") (🌙) has been a recurring symbol in a range of cultures since at least 3,000 BCE or possibly earlier with bull horns dating to the earliest [cave paintings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cave_painting "Cave painting") at 40,000 [BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Before_present "Before present").[&#8202;](#ref-back-e093-2)[\[255\]](#refnote-e093)[&#8202;](#ref-back-k874-1)[\[262\]](#refnote-k874) In [writing systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Writing_system "Writing system") such as Chinese the crescent has developed into the symbol [月](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%E6%9C%88 "wikt:月"), the word for Moon, and in ancient Egyptian it was the symbol [𓇹](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%F0%93%87%B9 "wikt:𓇹"), meaning Moon and spelled like the ancient Egyptian lunar deity _[Iah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iah "Iah")_,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hart_2005_p._77-0)[\[401\]](#refnote-Hart_2005_p._77) which the other ancient Egyptian lunar deities [Khonsu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khonsu "Khonsu") and [Thoth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thoth "Thoth") were associated with.[&#8202;](#ref-back-414-0)[\[402\]](#refnote-414)[&#8202;](#ref-back-415-0)[\[403\]](#refnote-415)

Iconographically the crescent was used in [Mesopotamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamia "Mesopotamia") as the primary symbol of [Nanna/Sîn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sin_(mythology) "Sin (mythology)"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-BlackGreen1992-1)[\[259\]](#refnote-BlackGreen1992) the ancient [Sumerian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumeria "Sumeria") lunar deity,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Nemet1998-0)[\[404\]](#refnote-Nemet1998)[&#8202;](#ref-back-BlackGreen1992-2)[\[259\]](#refnote-BlackGreen1992) who was the father of [Inanna/Ishtar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inanna "Inanna"), the goddess of the planet [Venus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venus "Venus") (symbolized as the [eight pointed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octagram "Octagram") [Star of Ishtar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_of_Ishtar "Star of Ishtar")),[&#8202;](#ref-back-Nemet1998-1)[\[404\]](#refnote-Nemet1998)[&#8202;](#ref-back-BlackGreen1992-3)[\[259\]](#refnote-BlackGreen1992) and [Utu/Shamash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utu "Utu"), the god of the Sun ( [symbolized as a disc, optionally with eight rays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_symbol "Solar symbol")),[&#8202;](#ref-back-Nemet1998-2)[\[404\]](#refnote-Nemet1998)[&#8202;](#ref-back-BlackGreen1992-4)[\[259\]](#refnote-BlackGreen1992) all three often depicted next to each other. Nanna/Sîn is, like some other lunar deities, for example Iah and Khonsu of ancient Egypt, [Mene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mene_(goddess) "Mene (goddess)")/ [Selene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selene "Selene") of ancient Greece and [Luna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_(mythology) "Luna (mythology)") of ancient Rome, depicted as a [horned deity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horned_deity "Horned deity"), featuring crescent shaped headgears or crowns.[&#8202;](#ref-back-417-0)[\[405\]](#refnote-417)[&#8202;](#ref-back-418-0)[\[406\]](#refnote-418)

The particular arrangement of the crescent with a star known as the [star and crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_and_crescent "Star and crescent") (☪️) goes back to the Bronze Age, representing either the Sun and Moon, or the Moon and the planet Venus, in combination. Since then the [heraldric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heraldry "Heraldry") use of the star and crescent proliferated, Byzantium's symbolism possibly influencing the development of the [Ottoman flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_flag "Ottoman flag"), specifically the combination of the Turkish crescent with a star,[&#8202;](#ref-back-419-0)[\[407\]](#refnote-419) and becoming a popular [symbol for Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symbols_of_Islam "Symbols of Islam") (as the _[hilal](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/hilal#Noun "wikt:hilal")_ of the [Islamic calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_calendar "Islamic calendar")) and [for a range of nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_and_crescent#Contemporary_use "Star and crescent").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kadoi_2014-0)[\[408\]](#refnote-Kadoi_2014)

#### Other associations

The features of the Moon, the contrasting brighter highlands and darker maria, have been seen by different cultures [forming abstract shapes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_pareidolia "Lunar pareidolia"). Such shapes are among others the [Man in the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_in_the_Moon "Man in the Moon") (e.g. [Coyolxāuhqui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coyolx%C4%81uhqui "Coyolxāuhqui")) or the [Moon Rabbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Rabbit "Moon Rabbit") (e.g. the Chinese [Tu'er Ye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu'er_Ye "Tu'er Ye") or in [Indigenous American mythologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mythologies_of_the_indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas "Mythologies of the indigenous peoples of the Americas") the aspect of the [Maya moon goddess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maya_moon_goddess "Maya moon goddess"), from which possibly [Awilix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Awilix "Awilix") is derived, or of [Metztli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metztli "Metztli")/ [Tēcciztēcatl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C4%93ccizt%C4%93catl "Tēcciztēcatl")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-Collections_Search_–_Museum_of_Fine_Arts,_Boston_1987-0)[\[400\]](#refnote-Collections_Search_–_Museum_of_Fine_Arts,_Boston_1987)

Throughout the global Middle Ages, the Moon held an important cultural role in topics as wide-ranging as [love poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_poetry "Love poetry"), [prophecy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prophecy "Prophecy"), medical thinking.[&#8202;](#ref-back-421-0)[\[409\]](#refnote-421) In western [alchemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alchemy "Alchemy"), the Moon was associated with [silver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver "Silver"), while gold was associated with the Sun.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Abbri_2019_pp._39–44-0)[\[410\]](#refnote-Abbri_2019_pp._39–44)

### Representation in modern culture

See also: [Moon in science fiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_in_science_fiction "Moon in science fiction") and [List of appearances of the Moon in fiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_appearances_of_the_Moon_in_fiction "List of appearances of the Moon in fiction")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Van_Gogh_-_Starry_Night_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/500px-Van_Gogh_-_Starry_Night_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Van_Gogh_-_Starry_Night_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg)

The Moon is prominently featured in [Vincent van Gogh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_van_Gogh "Vincent van Gogh")'s 1889 painting _[The Starry Night](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Starry_Night "The Starry Night")_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_New_York_Times_2019-0)[\[411\]](#refnote-The_New_York_Times_2019)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Melies_color_Voyage_dans_la_lune.jpg/500px-Melies_color_Voyage_dans_la_lune.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Melies_color_Voyage_dans_la_lune.jpg)

An iconic image of the [Man in the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_in_the_Moon "Man in the Moon") from the first [science-fiction film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science-fiction_film "Science-fiction film") set in space, _[A Trip to the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Trip_to_the_Moon "A Trip to the Moon")_ (1902, [Georges Méliès](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_M%C3%A9li%C3%A8s "Georges Méliès")), inspired by a history of literature about going to the Moon.

The perception of the Moon in the [modern era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_era "Modern era") has been informed by [telescope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telescope "Telescope")-enabled [modern astronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_astronomy "Modern astronomy"). These new insights inspired cultural references, connecting romantic reflections about the Moon[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_MIT_Press_Reader_2020-0)[\[412\]](#refnote-The_MIT_Press_Reader_2020) and speculative fiction such as science-fiction dealing with the Moon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_New_York_Times_2019-1)[\[411\]](#refnote-The_New_York_Times_2019)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Nature_2019-0)[\[413\]](#refnote-Nature_2019)

Later [spaceflight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spaceflight "Spaceflight") ushered in the [space age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_age "Space age"), which produced especially with the first [Moon landings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_landing "Moon landing") historic achievements and references for [global society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_society "Global society"), represented through [images of Earth from a distance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_image_of_Earth "First image of Earth") and summarized in an awe and awareness called the [overview effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overview_effect "Overview effect"), amounting to [Moon joy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_joy "Moon joy") by reaching the Moon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Mashable_20260410-0)[\[414\]](#refnote-Mashable_20260410)

Video of the first step on the surface of the Moon, performed by [Neil Armstrong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neil_Armstrong "Neil Armstrong") and commented with "That's one small step for \[a\] man, one giant leap for mankind.", which was broadcast live, internationally (1969, [Apollo 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11 "Apollo 11")).

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/A_Man_on_the_Moon%2C_AS11-40-5903.jpg/500px-A_Man_on_the_Moon%2C_AS11-40-5903.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:A_Man_on_the_Moon,_AS11-40-5903.jpg)

The much reproduced image of [Buzz Aldrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buzz_Aldrin "Buzz Aldrin") (Apollo 11), with Neil Armstrong and Earth reflected in his visor.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Byrne._2019-0)[\[415\]](#refnote-Byrne._2019)

This has been accompanied
by public and critical reflection on humanity's cultural and [legal relation to the celestial body](#Legal_status), particularly as an object of [imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperialism "Imperialism"), as in the [space race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_race "Space race"), and as an object of [colonialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_colonization#Colonialism "Space colonization") through [lunar resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_resources "Lunar resources") [commercialization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercialization_of_space "Commercialization of space").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Alvarez_2020_p.-2)[\[343\]](#refnote-Alvarez_2020_p.) The 1970 poem " [Whitey on the Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitey_on_the_Moon "Whitey on the Moon")" has become famous for its expression of such critical reflections.[&#8202;](#ref-back-x071-0)[\[416\]](#refnote-x071) Other reflections have invoked the Moon's nature,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Declaration-1)[\[386\]](#refnote-Declaration) raising issues like lunar conservation and [sustainability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_sustainability "Space sustainability"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-Nast_2013-1)[\[345\]](#refnote-Nast_2013) understanding the Moon as a [common](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commons "Commons").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Open_Lunar_Foundation_2022-0)[\[417\]](#refnote-Open_Lunar_Foundation_2022)[&#8202;](#ref-back-The_Space_Review_2021-1)[\[380\]](#refnote-The_Space_Review_2021)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Evans_2021-1)[\[388\]](#refnote-Evans_2021)

In 2021 the 20 July became the _International Moon Day_, the date of the [first crewed Moon landing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11#Landing "Apollo 11").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Nations_1967_a849-0)[\[418\]](#refnote-Nations_1967_a849)

### Lunar effect

Main article: [Lunar effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunar_effect "Lunar effect")

The lunar effect is a supposed correlation between specific stages of the roughly 29.5-day lunar cycle, and behavior and physiological changes in humans on Earth. Lunar cycles have significant impacts on human culture, but no solid evidence connects these cycles to human biology.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FosterRoenneberg2008-0)[\[419\]](#refnote-FosterRoenneberg2008)

The Moon has long been associated with insanity and irrationality; the words _lunacy_ and _[lunatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunatic "Lunatic")_ are derived from the Latin name for the Moon, _Luna_. Philosophers [Aristotle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristotle "Aristotle") and [Pliny the Elder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pliny_the_Elder "Pliny the Elder") argued that the full moon induced insanity in susceptible individuals, believing that the brain, which is mostly water, must be affected by the Moon and its power over the tides, but the Moon's gravity is too slight to affect any single person.[&#8202;](#ref-back-sciam-0)[\[420\]](#refnote-sciam)
In modern society lunar effects have been claimed to be responsible for supposed increased admissions to psychiatric hospitals, traffic accidents, homicides or suicides during a full moon,[&#8202;](#ref-back-sciam-1)[\[420\]](#refnote-sciam) but these claims have been invalidated by at least 37 studies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-RottonKelly1985-0)[\[421\]](#refnote-RottonKelly1985)

## Explanatory notes

01. [&#8202;](#refnote-inclination)[↑](#ref-back-inclination-0)Between 18.29° and 28.58° to Earth's [equator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equator "Equator")[&#8202;](#ref-back-W06-11)[\[1\]](#refnote-W06)
02. [&#8202;](#refnote-near-Earth_asteroids)[↑](#ref-back-near-Earth_asteroids-0)There are a number of [near-Earth asteroids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near-Earth_asteroid "Near-Earth asteroid"), including [3753 Cruithne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3753_Cruithne "3753 Cruithne"), that are [co-orbital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-orbital "Co-orbital") with Earth: their orbits bring them close to Earth for periods of time but then alter in the long term (Morais et al, 2002). These are [quasi-satellites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quasi-satellite "Quasi-satellite")– they are not moons as they do not orbit Earth. For more information, see [Other moons of Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Other_moons_of_Earth "Other moons of Earth").

03. [&#8202;](#refnote-maxval)[↑](#ref-back-maxval-0)The _maximum value_ is given based on scaling of the brightness from the value of −12.74 given for an equator to Moon-center distance of 378000km in the NASA factsheet reference to the minimum Earth–Moon distance given there, after the latter is corrected for Earth's equatorial radius of 6378km, giving 350600km. The _minimum value_ (for a distant [new moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_moon "New moon")) is based on a similar scaling using the maximum Earth–Moon distance of 407000km (given in the factsheet) and by calculating the brightness of the [earthshine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthshine "Earthshine") onto such a new moon. The brightness of the earthshine is \[ Earth [albedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albedo "Albedo") ×( [Earth radius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earth_radius "Earth radius") / Radius of [Moon's orbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon's_orbit "Moon's orbit"))2\] relative to the direct solar illumination that occurs for a full moon. (Earth albedo = 0.367; Earth radius = (polar radius× equatorial radius)½ = 6 367 km.)

04. [&#8202;](#refnote-angular_size)[↑](#ref-back-angular_size-0)The range of angular size values given are based on simple scaling of the following values given in the fact sheet reference: at an Earth-equator to Moon-center distance of 378000km, the [angular size](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angular_size "Angular size") is 1896 [arcseconds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcsecond "Arcsecond"). The same fact sheet gives extreme Earth–Moon distances of 407000km and 357000km. For the maximum angular size, the minimum distance has to be corrected for Earth's equatorial radius of 6378km, giving 350600km.

05. [&#8202;](#refnote-pressure_explanation)[↑](#ref-back-pressure_explanation-0)Lucey et al. (2006) give 107 particles cm−3 by day and 105 particles cm−3 by night. Along with equatorial surface temperatures of 390 [K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelvin "Kelvin") by day and 100K by night, the [ideal gas law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ideal_gas_law "Ideal gas law") yields the pressures given in the infobox (rounded to the nearest [order of magnitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_magnitude "Order of magnitude")): 10−7 [Pa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pascal_(unit) "Pascal (unit)") by day and 10−10Pa by night.

06. [&#8202;](#refnote-85)[↑](#ref-back-85-0)There is no strong correlation between the sizes of planets and the sizes of their satellites. Larger planets tend to have more satellites, both large and small, than smaller planets.
07. [&#8202;](#refnote-Moon_vs._Charon)[↑](#ref-back-Moon_vs._Charon-0)With 27% the diameter and 60% the density of Earth, the Moon has 1.23% of the mass of Earth. The moon [Charon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charon_(moon) "Charon (moon)") is larger relative to its primary [Pluto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluto "Pluto"), but Earth and the Moon are different since Pluto is considered a [dwarf planet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwarf_planet "Dwarf planet") and not a planet, unlike Earth.

08. [&#8202;](#refnote-distance_ratio)[↑](#ref-back-distance_ratio-0)The mean distance of the Moon from the Earth is 384401 km. The equatorial radius of Earth is 6.378136⋅106 [meters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre "Metre"). Multiplying by 2π to convert from equatorial radius to equatorial circumference results in 4.007501⋅107 [meters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre "Metre"), or 40075.01km. Dividing the two results in a ratio of 9.59.

09. [&#8202;](#refnote-orbpd)[↑](#ref-back-orbpd-0)More accurately, the Moon's mean sidereal period (fixed star to fixed star) is 27.321661days (27 d 07 h 43 min 11.5 s), and its mean tropical orbital period (from equinox to equinox) is 27.321582days (27 d 07 h 43 min 04.7 s) ( _Explanatory Supplement to the Astronomical Ephemeris_, 1961, at p.107).

10. [&#8202;](#refnote-synpd)[↑](#ref-back-synpd-0)More accurately, the Moon's mean synodic period (between mean solar conjunctions) is 29.530589days (29 d 12 h 44 min 02.9 s) ( _Explanatory Supplement to the Astronomical Ephemeris_, 1961, at p.107).

11. [&#8202;](#refnote-size_changes)[↑](#ref-back-size_changes-0)See graph in [Sun#Life phases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun#Life_phases "Sun"). At present, the diameter of the Sun is increasing at a rate of about five percent per billion years. This is very similar to the rate at which the apparent angular diameter of the Moon is decreasing as it recedes from Earth.

12. [&#8202;](#refnote-area)[↑](#ref-back-area-0)On average, the Moon covers an area of 0.21078 square degrees on the night sky.

106. [1](#ref-back-Tikoo-0) [2](#ref-back-Tikoo-1) [3](#ref-back-Tikoo-2)Tikoo, Sonia M.; Weiss, Benjamin P.; Shuster, David L.; Suavet, Clément; Wang, Huapei; Grove, Timothy L. (August 4, 2017). ["A two-billion-year history for the lunar dynamo"](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC5550224). _Science Advances_. **3** (8) e1700207. [Bibcode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibcode_(identifier) "Bibcode (identifier)"): [2017SciA....3E0207T](https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/2017SciA....3E0207T). [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_(identifier) "Doi (identifier)"): [10.1126/sciadv.1700207](https://doi.org/10.1126%2Fsciadv.1700207). [ISSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISSN_(identifier) "ISSN (identifier)") [2375-2548](https://search.worldcat.org/issn/2375-2548). [PMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMC_(identifier) "PMC (identifier)") [5550224](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC5550224). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_(identifier) "PMID (identifier)") [28808679](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28808679). No single dynamo mechanism proposed thus far can explain the strong fields inferred for the period before 3.56 Ga while also allowing the dynamo to persist in such a weakened state beyond ~2.5 Ga. Therefore, our results suggest that the dynamo was powered by at least two distinct mechanisms operating during early and late lunar history.

[PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_(identifier) "PMID (identifier)") [25477467](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25477467). It has now been established that a dynamo magnetic field likely existed on the Moon from at least 4.5 billion to 3.56 billion years ago, with an intensity similar to that at the surface of Earth today.
108. [↑](#ref-back-115-0)Mighani, Saied; Wang, Huapei; Shuster, David L.; Borlina, Cauȇ S.; Nichols, Claire I. O.; Weiss, Benjamin P. (January 3, 2020). [PMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMC_(identifier) "PMC (identifier)") [6938704](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC6938704). [PMID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PMID_(identifier) "PMID (identifier)") [31911941](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31911941). However, paleomagnetic measurements of Apollo samples indicate that the Moon once generated a core dynamo with surface field intensities of several tens of microtesla (comparable to that of Earth today) during the period 4.25 to 3.56 billion years (Ga) ago (2–7). Following this high-field epoch, the field declined by at least an order of magnitude by 3.2 Ga ago (8) and persisted in a weakened state (~5 μT) until at least 2.5 Ga ago (9).
